    "text": "Warning: truncated output (original token count: 40028)\nTotal output lines: 1249\n\nWarning: truncated output (original token count: 52405)\nTotal output lines: 1636\n\nCELL 1\n# **Module 4 &mdash; Perceptron and Linear Separability**\r\nCELL 2\n## **4.1 A Biological Neuron<font color='#FFCC00'>  &#x2B24; </font>**\n\n\n![Biological neuron illustration](https://docs.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1EPKYKcOpGw3hHTAlBw5wKfqiSWK5bOef)\n\nBiologists have studied animal neurons. A typical neuron works by taking a number of inputs that are chemical/electrical signals from other neurons through its dendrites. Not all incoming signals have the same 'weight': the brain is plastic and the dendrites modulate the signals before they are combined in the cell nucleus. When the combined modulated signals exceed some threshold (which may be different for each neuron), the neuron becomes *activated* and sends a signal to its output. Otherwise, the neuron is not activated.\n\nSince the neuron appears to have two fundamental states (activated or not), we can speculate that it may be able to make binary decisions or classifications. This leads us to the mathematical modeling of a biological neuron as a perceptron.\n\r\nCELL 3\n## **4.2 Perceptron<font color='#FF5733'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nWe assume that the neuron/perceptron has $d$ inputs $x=[x_1,\\ldots,x_d]$ and each input is modulated, i.e. multiplied by a corresponding weight $w = [w_1,\\ldots,w_d]$. Thus what we call the **net input** is given by the following equation:\n\n$$\n    z = wx^\\top =  \\sum_{j=1}^d w_j x_j\\, .\n$$\n\nThe neuron then has an **activation function** $\\phi(z)$ defined as follows for some activation threshold $\\theta\\,$:\n\n\n*   $\\phi(z) =1$, if $z\\geq \\theta\\:$ **or**  $\\:\\phi(z) = 0$, if $z<\\theta$ .\n\nAnother way to state the above rule is to set $b = -\\theta$ and write it as follows:\n*   $\\phi(z) =1$, if $wx^\\top + b\\geq 0\\:$ **or**  $\\:\\phi(z) = 0$, if $wx^\\top+b< 0$ .\n\n\n<br>\n\n**Some additional notation**. For conciseness, we can set $w_0=b$ and let $w = [w_0,w_1,\\ldots,w_d]$. Then, we can assume that all our points have an extra 'fixed' coordinate and they look like this: $x=[1,x_1,\\ldots, x_d]$. In that case the rule becomes:\n\n*   $\\phi(z) =1$, if $wx^\\top \\geq 0\\:$ **or**  $\\:\\phi(z) = 0$, if $wx^\\top < 0$ .\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\n\r\nCELL 4\n## **4.3 Linear Separability<font color='#FF5733'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nIt is very useful to think about the points $x$ at which the perceptron is undecided, i.e. *on the fence* between the two decisions.\nWorking with the definition, these are exactly the set of points $x$ that satisfy\n\n$$ wx^\\top + b =0\\, .$$\n\nThis decision boundary, or **decision surface**, is the equation of a $d$-dimensional hyperplane, and the two decision regions will be what we call half-spaces.\n\n![linear separatility in 2D](https://docs.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1x9RwkW-cQxfMo7_G3oV6KermBVUjxpXI)\n\nFor a 2D example, the boundary is a line, while the two semi-planes are the two decision regions.\n\nThis leads us to the notion of **linear separability**: If we have a dataset $X$ where datapoints belong to two classes, and these two classes can be separated by a hyperplane, then we say that $X$ is linearly separable.\n\nIt follows, by definition, that if $X$ is linearly separable then there exists a perceptron that can separate it (and be 100% accurate on all points). And vice-versa, if $X$ is not linearly separable, then no perceptron can fully separate it.\n\r\nCELL 5\n## **4.4  The Perceptron Algorithm<font color='#008DFF'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nWe now want to come up with an algorithm that will learn the weights $w,b$ of a perceptron, so as to separate the classes of $X$ when $X$ is linearly separable. Recall that $X$ comes with a set of categorical labels $y$, and in this case we can set these labels to the numerical values $0$, $1$ so that they coincide with the possible outputs of the perceptron.\n\nHere is the algorithm:\n\n---\n*   **function** $w$ = Perceptron-fit ($X$,$y$)\n*   Randomly initialize a $d$-dimensional vector $w$ and a scalar $b$\n*   $\\mathit{errorFlag} = \\mathrm{True}$\n*   **while** $\\mathit{errorFlag}$ **do**    &nbsp; &nbsp; <font color='#008DFF'> #new epoch starts here </font>\n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; $\\mathit{errorFlag} = \\mathrm{False}$\n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; **for** $j=1$ **to** $n$:\n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; $z = b + X_j w^\\top$\n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; **if** &nbsp; $z\\geq 0$ &nbsp; **then** &nbsp; $\\hat{y} = 1$ &nbsp; **else** &nbsp; $\\hat{y} = 0$  &nbsp; &nbsp;  <font color='#008DFF'> #in linear regression we use $\\hat{y} = z$ </font>\n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; **if** &nbsp; $\\hat{y} \\neq  y_j$ &nbsp; **then** &nbsp; $\\mathit{errorFlag} = \\mathrm{True}$\n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; $b  = b -  \\left( \\hat{y}  - y_j\\right ) $\n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; **for** $k=1$ **to** $d$:  \n*   &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp; &nbsp;\n     $w_k = w_k - \\left( \\hat{y} - y_j\\right) X_{j,k} $ &nbsp;\n*   **return** $w,b$\n---\n\nThe algorithm strategy is to learn from each mistake. When the perceptron makes a mistake on a point $X_j$, then the weights $w,b$ are updated, and in fact the update rules are precisely the rules we used in stochastic gradient descent (with $\\rho = 1$) for linear regression with one exception: $\\hat{y}$ is now a categorical value $\\{0,1\\}$, whereas in linear regression we were using $\\hat{y}=z$.\n\nLike gradient descent, many variants of the perceptron algorithm are possible, with different learning rates $\\rho$, stochastic variants and other variants involving batching, and so on. Note also that the algorithm can be run with other numerical values as class labels (such as $y,\\hat{y}\\in\\{-1,1\\}$).\n\nThe algorithm stops only when no $\\mathit{errorFlag}$ is raised during an epoch, i.e. when all points are classified correctly. But is that guaranteed to happen?\n\nYes, as it turns out.\n\n**Fact 1:** If $X$ is a linearly separable dataset, then the perceptron algorithm will eventually terminate.\n\nThere is an even stronger statement that can be made.\n\n**Fact 2:** If $X$ is a linearly separable dataset, then one can compute a number $M$ that depends only on $X$, such that the perceptron algorithm will make at most $M$ mistakes during its course.\n\nFact 2 is known as the Rosenblatt-Novikoff Theorem. Frank Rosenblatt was the person that in 1958 came up with the idea of the perceptron, which according to many paved the way for modern Machine Learning [[1](https://news.cornell.edu/stories/2019/09/professors-perceptron-paved-way-ai-60-years-too-soon)]. You can find a proof and some other cool things about the perceptron in this [link](http://ciml.info/dl/v0_99/ciml-v0_99-ch04.pdf).\n\n\n\r\nCELL 6\n## **4.5  One-vs-One for Multilabel Classification<font color='#FFCC00'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nWe now introduce the One-vs-One method for using perceptrons on multilabel data.\n\nWe will use a small example. Suppose that our data $X$ contains points with three labels: *dog*, *cat*, *human*. With this labeling, we can think of $X$ being partitioned into 3 submatrices: $X_{\\mathit{cat}}$, $X_{\\mathit{dog}}$, $X_{\\mathit{human}}$. For each pair of data classes, we can train a perceptron. More specifically, we train the following classifiers:\n\n* $C_1$, the cat-vs-dog classifier, using only data in $X_{\\mathit{cat}}$ and $X_{\\mathit{dog}}$.\n* $C_2$, the human-vs-dog classifier, using only data in $X_{\\mathit{human}}$ and $X_{\\mathit{dog}}$.\n* $C_3$, the human-vs-cat classifier, using only data in $X_{\\mathit{human}}$ and $X_{\\mathit{cat}}$.\n\nThen, if we want to predict the label of a new point $x$, we first generate three different predictions from the classifiers $C_1$, $C_2$, $C_3$.  Then we imagine a 'voting' process where each classifier votes for dog, human, or cat. The prediction that takes the most votes is the final prediction for $x$. If we have a tie, then we break it arbitrarily between the winners.\n\nMore generally, if we have data classes then we need to train ${k \\choose 2} = k(k-1)/2$ different classifiers and use the majority rule.\r\nCELL 7\n## **4.6  Learning Logical Gates<font color='#FFCC00'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nComputer hardware and CPUs in particular, consist of millions of simple transistors implementing three basic logical functions: AND, OR and NOT.\n\n![Truth tables for logical gates](https://docs.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1wRIUQyytudayzx9S94nGr0LaK4SKsR5T)\n\nAll other logical functions can be impemented using networks of these three basic gates. For example the XOR function (whose truth table is above) can be implemented as a 3-layer network of NOT-AND gates as shown here:\n\n![XOR implemented with NAND gates](https://docs.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1wX8S_kHuIxpsVLOzJ5JiQA3NXxXvkjoY)\n\n\n\n\r\nCELL 8\n\nOne way to see these logical functions (say the AND gate) is as a binary classifier that separates 4 points into two classes. Then one can ask this question: Can a perceptron 'learn' the 2-input OR gate? We can instantly see that the answer is yes by looking at the linear separability in the picture. Similarly, AND & NOT-AND are linearly separable.\n\n![Linear separability for logical gates](https://docs.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1wguGVcgldwMEa1RobiwH_wgyBqxZB6Z4)\n\nOn the other hand, the XOR logical gate cannot be learned by a simple perceptron, but as we will see we a network of perceptrons can 'learn' the XOR gate.\n\nSimilarly to logical circuits in computers , it is believed that the high-level functionality of our brain emerges through networks of neurons. But what type of computational tasks can individual neurons perform? It was until recently believed that individual human neurons can only behave like simple separable logical gates, but there is now evidence that single neurons can actually be more complex and operate even like XOR gates [[2](https://www.quantamagazine.org/neural-dendrites-reveal-their-computational-power-20200114/)].\r\nCELL 9\n## **4.6  Perceptron Code Notebook<font color='#369870'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nHere is the [code notebook](https://colab.research.google.com/drive/1aXWNLI5tIIWcbrr6mSk_YzO7CRWKul5Q?usp=drive_fs).\r\nCELL 10\n## **4.7  Feature Engineering Revisited<font color='#FFCC00'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nConsider the picture on the left. The dataset is clearly not separable, so it can not be learned by a perceptron.\n\nTo each point $(x,y)$ in our dataset, we now add one extra feature/coordinate, $x^2+y^2$, so that the point becomes $(x,y,x^2+y^2)$.\n\n![The non-linear power of engineered features](https://docs.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1B-SBFNy3OgR0XTkEWYTW8RgBHCWMc_eC)\n\nThis 'engineered' 3D-dataset is plotted in the second figure. We can see that the dataset has now become linearly separable. Specifically we see that the plane separating the two classes is defined by $x^2+y^2 = 0.5$; that is, the third coordinate is equal to 0.5. If we go back to the original space, the set of points $(x,y)$ that satisfy the equation $x^2+y^2 = 0.5$ is a circle of radius $1/\\sqrt{2}$. The linear separation surface in 3D becomes a non-linear surface in 2D.  \n\nThis is yet another example of the power of feature engineering. However, generally speaking, engineering appropriate features is a difficult task that requires a very good understanding of the application and/or the dataset.\n\r\nCELL 11\n## **4.8 Perceptron Weaknesses<font color='#FFCC00'> &#x2B24; </font>**\n\nWe can now identify some weaknesses of the perceptron:\n\n* Given a linearly separable dataset $X$, the number of perceptrons that can separate the classes is usually infinite. The output of the perceptron learning algorithm depends on the random initialization and the order in which the points are visited by the algorithm.\n* In some cases, the algorithm can learn a perceptron with a decision surface that is much closer to one of the classes than it is to the other. That is demonstrated in the following figure. Intuitively speaking, such decision surfaces are undesirable since we should not have any *a priori* preference for any class.\n\n\n\n![A linearly separable dataset](https://docs.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1x9RwkW-cQxfMo7_G3oV6KermBVUjxpXI)\r\nCELL 12\n## **References**\n\n[1] [Professor's perceptron paved the way for AI &mdash; 60 years too soon](https://news.cornell.edu/stories/2019/09/professors-perceptron-paved-way-ai-60-years-too-soon) <br>\n[2] [Hidden computational power found in the arms of neurons](https://www.quantamagazine.org/neural-dendrites-reveal-their-computational-power-20200114/)\r\nD:\\GitHub\\ML NJIT\\TranscriptClasses\\Week 3\\Class A.txt-17-Michael E.    out.innerHTML=plot(regions+ns.map(n=>`<path d="M${sx(q[0])} ${sy(q[1])}L${sx(n.x[0])} ${sy(n.x[1])}" stroke="#526d73" stroke-dasharray="4 4"/>`).join('')+data.map((n,i)=>dot(n.x[0],n.x[1],reg?'#127e80':n.y?'#9c68b4':'#127e80',ns.some(v=>v.i===i)?7:4)).join('')+`<path d="M${sx(q[0])-8} ${sy(q[1])}h16 M${sx(q[0])} ${sy(q[1])-8}v16" stroke="#e36443" stroke-width="4"/>`,'Vecinos de la consulta, cruz naranja, y regiones de clasificación')+`<div class="lab-stats">${stat('Predicción',reg?fmt(pred):'Clase '+pred)}${stat('Vecinos',k)}${stat('Distancia',p===Infinity?'L∞':'L'+p)}</div><p class="caption">Verde: clase 0 · Violeta: clase 1 · Cruz naranja: consulta. ${reg?'Targets sintéticos: y = 2x₁ + x₂.':'El fondo muestra la clase predicha. Los puntos grandes son los vecinos seleccionados.'} Empates de voto: clase menor; de distancia: orden de los puntos.</p>`+table(['Vecino','Distancia','Target'],ns.map(n=>['('+n.x.join(', ')+')',fmt(n.d),n.y]));};
 }else if(id==='distances'){
  controls.innerHTML=select('p','Norma',[['1','L₁ · rombo'],['2','L₂ · círculo'],['4','L₄'],['Infinity','L∞ · cuadrado']])+slider('dx','Diferencia |Δx₁|',0,5,.1,3)+slider('dy','Diferencia |Δx₂|',0,5,.1,4)+slider('sigma','σ · similitud gaussiana',.1,5,.1,2);
  draw=()=>{const p=value('p'),x=value('dx'),y=value('dy'),sigma=value('sigma');let pts=[];for(let t=0;t<=Math.PI*2+.01;t+=.025){let a=Math.cos(t),b=Math.sin(t),norm=p===Infinity?Math.max(Math.abs(a),Math.abs(b)):(Math.abs(a)**p+Math.abs(b)**p)**(1/p);pts.push(`${300+120*a/norm},${170-120*b/norm}`);}out.innerHTML=svg(`<path d="M100 170H500 M300 15V325" stroke="var(--muted)"/><polyline points="${pts.join(' ')}" fill="#bce3df88" stroke="#127e80" stroke-width="3"/><text x="430" y="192">1</text><text x="310" y="45">1</text><text x="300" y="352" text-anchor="middle">Frontera a distancia 1 del origen · L${p===Infinity?'∞':p}</text>`,'Bola unitaria de la norma seleccionada')+`<div class="lab-stats">${stat('L₁',fmt(x+y))}${stat('L₂',fmt(Math.hypot(x,y)))}${stat('L∞',fmt(Math.max(x,y)))}</div><p>Para Δ=(${x}, ${y}), L₂ = √(${fmt(x*x)} + ${fmt(y*y)}).</p><p>Similitud gaussiana: exp(−${fmt(x*x+y*y)}/(2·${sigma}²)) = <strong>${fmt(Math.exp(-(x*x+y*y)/(2*sigma*sigma)))}</strong>.</p><p class="caption">La curva representa la bola unitaria; los controles Δ calculan un ejemplo separado. La similitud usa L₂ independientemente de la norma dibujada.</p>`;};
 }else if(id==='regression'){
  controls.innerHTML=slider('a','Pendiente · a',-.5,2,.05,.6)+slider('b','Intercepto · b',-2,4,.1,1)+select('outlier','Observación atípica',[['0','Sin outlier'],['1','Cambiar último target a 2']]);
  draw=()=>{const a=value('a'),b=value('b');const pts=demoPoints.map((v,i)=>({...v,y:value('outlier')&&i===7?2:v.y}));out.innerHTML=plot(`<defs><clipPath id="graph-clip"><rect x="45" y="20" width="525" height="290"/></clipPath></defs><g clip-path="url(#graph-clip)">${pts.map(v=>`<path d="M${sx(v.x)} ${sy(v.y)}V${sy(a*v.x+b)}" stroke="#df7859" stroke-dasharray="4 3"/>`).join('')}<path d="M${sx(0)} ${sy(b)}L${sx(10)} ${sy(10*a+b)}" stroke="#127e80" stroke-width="3"/>${pts.map(v=>dot(v.x,v.y)).join('')}</g>`,'Recta y residuos verticales')+`<div class="lab-stats">${stat('MSE',fmt(mse(pts,a,b)))}${stat('R²',fmt(r2(pts,a,b)))}${stat('Modelo',a+'x + '+b)}</div>`+table(['x','y real','ŷ','(ŷ − y)²'],pts.map(v=>[v.x,v.y,fmt(a*v.x+b),fmt((a*v.x+b-v.y)**2)]))+`<p class="caption">Ajusta la recta para reducir MSE y después activa el outlier. Las cifras evalúan estos mismos ocho puntos, no una prueba de generalización.</p>`;};
 }else if(id==='gradient'){
  let a=0,b=0,iteration=0,history=[mse(demoPoints,0,0)],message='';
  controls.innerHTML=slider('rate','Learning rate · η',.001,.1,.001,.01)+`<div class="button-row"><button class="primary" id="one">Dar un paso</button><button class="secondary" id="ten">10 pasos</button><button class="secondary" id="reset">Reiniciar</button></div><p class="caption">Full batch sobre 8 puntos. El gradiente mantiene el factor 2/n. Una tasa alta puede divergir.</p>`;
  draw=()=>{const g=gradient(demoPoints,a,b);const max=Math.max(...history,1);out.innerHTML=svg(`<rect x="45" y="20" width="525" height="290" rx="8" fill="var(--plot)"/><path d="M45 20V310H570" stroke="var(--muted)" fill="none"/><polyline fill="none" stroke="#127e80" stroke-width="3" points="${history.map((v,i)=>`${45+i/Math.max(history.length-1,1)*525},${310-v/max*270}`).join(' ')}"/><text x="50" y="16">MSE · máximo ${fmt(max)}</text><text x="300" y="346" text-anchor="middle">Actualizaciones: 0 → ${iteration}</text>`,'Evolución de MSE con cada paso de gradiente')+`<div class="lab-stats">${stat('Paso',iteration)}${stat('MSE',fmt(history.at(-1)))}${stat('a / b',fmt(a)+' / '+fmt(b))}</div><div class="calculation">∂L/∂a = ${fmt(g[0])}<br>∂L/∂b = ${fmt(g[1])}<br>a siguiente = ${fmt(a)} − ${value('rate')} · (${fmt(g[0])})<br>b siguiente = ${fmt(b)} − ${value('rate')} · (${fmt(g[1])})</div><p role="status">${message||'Los dos gradientes se calculan antes de modificar a y b.'}</p>`;};
  const advance=n=>{for(let i=0;i<n;i++){const [na,nb]=step(demoPoints,a,b,value('rate'));const loss=mse(demoPoints,na,nb);if(!Number.isFinite(loss)||loss>1e12){message='Divergencia: se detuvo el cálculo antes de exceder el límite numérico. Reduce η y reinicia.';break;}a=na;b=nb;iteration++;history.push(loss);message=loss>history.at(-2)?'La pérdida aumentó. Observa la tasa de aprendizaje.':'';}draw();};controls.querySelector('#one').onclick=()=>advance(1);controls.querySelector('#ten').onclick=()=>advance(10);controls.querySelector('#reset').onclick=()=>{a=b=iteration=0;history=[mse(demoPoints,0,0)];message='';draw();};
 }else if(id==='matrix'){
  controls.innerHTML=slider('w1','Peso w₁',-4,4,1,2)+slider('w2','Peso w₂',-4,4,1,-1)+slider('mb','Intercepto b',-3,3,1,1);
  draw=()=>{const w1=value('w1'),w2=value('w2'),b=value('mb');out.innerHTML=`<div class="matrix-diagram"><div><small>X · 2 × 2</small><pre>┌ 1  2 ┐
└ 3  4 ┘</pre></div><strong>×</strong><div><small>w · 2 × 1</small><pre>┌ ${w1} ┐
└ ${w2} ┘</pre></div><strong>+</strong><div><small>b · 1</small><pre>┌ ${b} ┐
└ ${b} ┘</pre></div></div><h3>Fila por columna</h3><div class="calculation">ŷ₁ = 1 × (${w1}) + 2 × (${w2}) + (${b}) = <b>${w1+2*w2+b}</b><br>ŷ₂ = 3 × (${w1}) + 4 × (${w2}) + (${b}) = <b>${3*w1+4*w2+b}</b></div><p>Las dimensiones interiores coinciden: (2 × <strong>2</strong>)(<strong>2</strong> × 1) → 2 × 1.</p><p class="caption">El resultado tiene una predicción por fila. Este es el mismo cálculo que usa un modelo de regresión con dos características.</p>`;};
 }else if(id==='scaling'){
  controls.innerHTML=slider('newx','Nueva observación x',0,12,.5,8);
  draw=()=>{const mean=4,sigma=Math.sqrt(8/3),x=value('newx');out.innerHTML=`<h3>Aprender la escala de train</h3>${table(['Etapa','Cálculo','Resultado'],[['Media μ','(2 + 4 + 6) / 3',4],['Varianza','(4 + 0 + 4) / 3',fmt(8/3)],['Desviación σ','√(8/3)',fmt(sigma)]])}<div class="lab-stats">${[2,4,6].map(v=>stat('Train x = '+v,fmt((v-mean)/sigma))).join('')}</div><div class="calculation">z nuevo = (${x} − 4) / ${fmt(sigma)} = <strong>${fmt((x-mean)/sigma)}</strong></div><p>El dato nuevo cambia; μ y σ se mantienen. Reajustar el escalador con la nueva observación alteraría las coordenadas aprendidas.</p>`;};
 }else{
  controls.innerHTML=slider('dim','Dimensión · d',2,1000,1,10)+slider('ratio','Radio interior / exterior',.5,.99,.01,.9);
  draw=()=>{const d=value('dim'),ratio=value('ratio'),inside=ratio**d;out.innerHTML=svg(`<circle cx="300" cy="165" r="130" fill="#e9d9ee" stroke="#a877b9" stroke-width="2"/><circle cx="300" cy="165" r="${130*ratio}" fill="#c2e3df" stroke="#127e80" stroke-width="2"/><path d="M300 165H430" stroke="#324d52"/><text x="305" y="155">R = 1</text><text x="300" y="325" text-anchor="middle">Esquema en 2D · las proporciones se calculan en d=${d}</text>`,'Esquema de radios interior y exterior')+`<div class="lab-stats">${stat('Fracción interior',fmt(inside))}${stat('Cáscara',fmt((1-inside)*100)+'%')}</div><div class="calculation">(${ratio})<sup>${d}</sup> = ${inside.toExponential(5)}</div><p>Para un millón de puntos uniformes, el número esperado en el interior es <strong>${fmt(1e6*inside)}</strong>.</p><p class="caption">El dibujo no representa literalmente un objeto de ${d} dimensiones. La hipótesis uniforme no describe necesariamente datos reales.</p>`;};
 }
 controls.querySelectorAll('input,select').forEach(input=>input.addEventListener('input',()=>{const label=controls.querySelector('#'+input.id+'-value');if(label)label.textContent=input.value;draw();}));draw();
}

export function distance(a,b,p=2){
  const ds=a.map((v,i)=>Math.abs(v-b[i]));
  if(p===Infinity)return Math.max(...ds);
  return ds.reduce((s,v)=>s+v**p,0)**(1/p);
}
export function neighbors(points,q,k,p=2){return points.map((v,i)=>({...v,i,d:distance(v.x,q,p)})).sort((a,b)=>a.d-b.d||a.i-b.i).slice(0,k);}
export function predict(points,q,k,p=2,regression=false,weighted=false){
  let ns=neighbors(points,q,k,p);
  if(weighted&&ns.some(n=>n.d===0))ns=ns.filter(n=>n.d===0);
  const weight=n=>weighted&&n.d>0?1/n.d:1;
  if(regression)return ns.reduce((s,n)=>s+n.y*weight(n),0)/ns.reduce((s,n)=>s+weight(n),0);
  const votes={};ns.forEach(n=>votes[n.y]=(votes[n.y]||0)+weight(n));
  return Number(Object.keys(votes).sort((a,b)=>votes[b]-votes[a]||Number(a)-Number(b))[0]);
}
export function mse(points,a,b){return points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(a*v.x+b-v.y)**2,0)/points.length;}
export function gradient(points,a,b){const n=points.length;return [2/n*points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(a*v.x+b-v.y)*v.x,0),2/n*points.reduce((s,v)=>s+a*v.x+b-v.y,0)];}
export function step(points,a,b,rate){const [ga,gb]=gradient(points,a,b);return [a-rate*ga,b-rate*gb];}
export function r2(points,a,b){const mean=points.reduce((s,v)=>s+v.y,0)/points.length;const total=points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(v.y-mean)**2,0);return total===0?null:1-points.length*mse(points,a,b)/total;}
export const shellFraction=(ratio,d)=>1-ratio**d;

import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {distance,neighbors,predict,mse,gradient,step,r2,shellFraction} from '../src/math.js';
import {lessons,quizzes,sources,weeks} from '../src/content.js';
import katex from 'katex';
const close=(a,b)=>assert.ok(Math.abs(a-b)<1e-9,`${a} != ${b}`);
test('Distances reproduce the 3-4-5 triangle and L infinity',()=>{assert.equal(distance([0,0],[3,4],1),7);assert.equal(distance([0,0],[3,4]),5);assert.equal(distance([0,0],[3,4],Infinity),4);});
test('kNN uses strict majority; k=1 never returns a class with zero votes',()=>{const data=[0,0,1,0,1].map((y,i)=>({x:[i],y}));assert.equal(predict(data,[0],5),0);assert.equal(predict(data,[0],1),0);});
test('kNN exact coincidences, vote ties and regression',()=>{const data=[{x:[0],y:0},{x:[0],y:1},{x:[2],y:1}];assert.equal(predict(data,[0],3,2,false,true),0);close(predict(data,[0],3,2,true,true),.5);close(predict([{x:[0],y:8},{x:[1],y:10},{x:[2],y:12}],[1],3,2,true),10);assert.deepEqual(neighbors(data,[0],2).map(n=>n.i),[0,1]);});
test('Regression MSE and R2 have hand-calculated results',()=>{const data=[{x:0,y:1},{x:1,y:3},{x:2,y:5}];close(mse(data,1,2),2/3);close(r2(data,1,2),.75);close(r2(data,0,10),-18.375);assert.equal(r2([{x:0,y:1},{x:1,y:1}],0,1),null);});
test('Analytic gradient matches finite differences; update is simultaneous',()=>{const data=[{x:0,y:1},{x:1,y:3}];assert.deepEqual(gradient(data,0,0),[-3,-4]);const [a,b]=step(data,0,0,.1);close(a,.3);close(b,.4);close(mse(data,a,b),2.825);const h=1e-5,g=gradient(data,.6,.2);for(let i=0;i<2;i++){const plus=i===0?mse(data,.6+h,.2):mse(data,.6,.2+h);const minus=i===0?mse(data,.6-h,.2):mse(data,.6,.2-h);close((plus-minus)/(2*h),g[i]);}});
test('High dimension shell uses volume exponent',()=>{close(shellFraction(.9,10),1-.3486784401);close(shellFraction(.5,3),.875);});
test('All lessons have valid connections, sources, weeks, math and quiz references',()=>{const ids=new Set(lessons.map(l=>l.id));assert.equal(ids.size,lessons.length);for(const l of lessons){assert.ok(weeks.some(w=>w.id===l.week));assert.ok(l.code&&l.exercise&&l.solution&&l.steps.length);l.connections.forEach(id=>assert.ok(ids.has(id)));l.refs.forEach(([id])=>assert.ok(sources[id]));katex.renderToString(l.formula,{throwOnError:true});}assert.equal(new Set(quizzes.map(q=>q.id)).size,quizzes.length);for(const q of quizzes){assert.ok(ids.has(q.lesson));assert.ok(q.answer>=0&&q.answer<q.options.length);}});

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
# Run on the existing VPS as root. Only this site's paths are changed.
archive=${1:?archive path required}
expected=${2:?sha256 required}
release_id=${3:?release id required}
[[ "$archive" == /tmp/ds675-*.tgz && "$release_id" =~ ^ds675-[0-9-]+$ ]] || exit 2
printf '%s  %s\n' "$expected" "$archive" | sha256sum -c -
domain=ds675.automationfreelancer.com
release="/home/automationfreelancer/releases/$release_id"
site="/home/automationfreelancer/htdocs/$domain"
config="/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/$domain.conf"
if [[ -e "$site" && ! -L "$site" ]]; then echo 'Existing site is not a symlink; inspect before replacing.' >&2; exit 2; fi
[[ ! -e "$release" ]] || { echo 'Release already exists' >&2; exit 2; }
mkdir -p "$release"
tar -xzf "$archive" -C "$release"
test -f "$release/index.html"
test -f "$release/health.json"
chown -R automationfreelancer:automationfreelancer "$release"
find "$release" -type d -exec chmod 755 {} +
find "$release" -type f -exec chmod 644 {} +
previous=$(readlink "$site" || true)
mkdir -p /home/automationfreelancer/backups
backup="/home/automationfreelancer/backups/$release_id.nginx.conf"
[[ ! -f "$config" ]] || cp "$config" "$backup"
ln -s "$release" "$site.next"
chown -h automationfreelancer:automationfreelancer "$site.next"
mv -Tf "$site.next" "$site"
if [[ ! -f "/etc/letsencrypt/live/$domain/fullchain.pem" ]]; then
cat > "$config" <<'NGINX'
server {
 listen 80;
 listen [::]:80;
 server_name ds675.automationfreelancer.com;
 root /home/automationfreelancer/htdocs/ds675.automationfreelancer.com;
 index index.html;
 location ^~ /.well-known/acme-challenge/ { allow all; }
 location / { try_files $uri $uri/ =404; }
}
NGINX
nginx -t
systemctl reload nginx
 certbot certonly --webroot -w "$site" -d "$domain" --non-interactive --keep-until-expiring
fi
cat > "$config" <<'NGINX'
server {
 listen 80;
 listen [::]:80;
 server_name ds675.automationfreelancer.com;
 root /home/automationfreelancer/htdocs/ds675.automationfreelancer.com;
 location ^~ /.well-known/acme-challenge/ { allow all; }
 location / { return 301 https://$host$request_uri; }
}
server {
 listen 443 ssl;
 listen [::]:443 ssl;
 server_name ds675.automationfreelancer.com;
 ssl_certificate /etc/letsencrypt/live/ds675.automationfreelancer.com/fullchain.pem;
 ssl_certificate_key /etc/letsencrypt/live/ds675.automationfreelancer.com/privkey.pem;
 include /etc/letsencrypt/options-ssl-nginx.conf;
 ssl_dhparam /etc/letsencrypt/ssl-dhparams.pem;
 root /home/automationfreelancer/htdocs/ds675.automationfreelancer.com;
 index index.html;
 autoindex off;

function gradient(points,a,b){const n=points.length;return [2/n*points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(a*v.x+b-v.y)*v.x,0),2/n*points.reduce((s,v)=>s+a*v.x+b-v.y,0)];}\nexport function step(points,a,b,rate){const total=points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(v.y-mean)**2,0);return total===0?null:1-points.length*mse(points,a,b)/total;}\nexport const shellFraction=(ratio,d)=>1-ratio**d;\n\r\nimport test from function weekPage(id){const w=weeks.find(w=>w.id===Number(id));if(!w)return missing();const ls=lessons.filter(l=>l.week===w.id);return heading(`SEMANA ${String(w.id).padStart(2,'0')} · ${w.subtitle}`,w.title,w.description)+`<div class="two-grid"><section class="panel"><h3>Al terminar podrás…</h3><ul class="check-list">${w.objectives.map(x=>`<li>${x}</li>`).join('')}</ul></section><section class="panel tinted"><span class="eyebrow">EL HILO CONDUCTOR</span><p>${w.summary}</p><a href="#/practice/${w.id}">Practicar esta semana →</a></section></div><p class="info-note">Organización según syllabus, pág. 5: M1 y M2 corresponden a semana 1; M3 a semana 2. Tu lista de videos ubica M2 en Week 2, por lo que también está accesible aquí como repaso.</p>${w.id===2?'<a class="inline-link" href="#/lesson/knn">↶ Repasar kNN y distancias de la semana 1</a>':''}<div class="section-top"><h2>Lecciones</h2><span>${ls.length} temas · ${ls.reduce((s,l)=>s+l.minutes,0)} min orientativos</span></div><div class="lesson-list">${ls.map(lessonLink).join('')}</div><section class="panel review-panel"><h3>Comprueba tu comprensión</h3>${w.review.map((x,i)=>`<label class="review-item"><input type="checkbox" data-check="w${id}-${i}" ${state.checks.includes('w'+id+'-'+i)?'checked':''}>${x}</label>`).join('')}<div class="button-row"><a class="primary" href="#/practice/${id}">Hacer el quiz →</a><a class="secondary" href="#/cheats">Consultar fórmulas</a></div></section>`;}
function lessonPage(id){const l=lessons.find(l=>l.id===id);if(!l)return missing();state.recent=[id,...state.recent.filter(x=>x!==id)].slice(0,8);save();const index=lessons.indexOf(l),next=lessons[index+1];return `<a class="back-link" href="#/week/${l.week}">← Semana ${l.week}</a>`+heading(`MODULE ${l.module} · ${l.minutes} MIN DE ESTUDIO ORIENTATIVOS`,l.title,l.subtitle)+`<div class="lesson-layout"><article class="lesson-body"><section id="intuition"><span class="eyebrow">01 · QUÉ ES E INTUICIÓN</span><p class="lead">${l.intuition}</p><h3>Por qué importa</h3><p>${l.why}</p></section><section id="formula"><span class="eyebrow">02 · LAS MATEMÁTICAS</span><div class="formula">${math(l.formula)}</div><p class="symbols">${l.symbols}</p></section><section id="example"><span class="eyebrow">03 · PASO A PASO</span><ol class="steps">${l.steps.map(x=>`<li>${x}</li>`).join('')}</ol></section>${l.lab?`<section id="experiment"><span class="eyebrow">04 · EXPLORA LA IDEA</span><div id="embedded-lab"></div></section>`:''}<section id="python"><span class="eyebrow">${l.lab?'05':'04'} · IMPLEMENTACIÓN PYTHON</span><div class="code-block"><div><span>Python · ejemplo de estudio</span><button class="copy" data-copy="${id}">Copiar código</button></div><pre><code>${escape(l.code)}</code></pre></div><p>${l.codeNotes}</p></section><section id="mistakes"><span class="eyebrow">EVITA ESTAS CONFUSIONES</span><div class="warning-box"><ul>${l.mistakes.map(x=>`<li>${x}</li>`).join('')}</ul></div></section><section id="exercise"><span class="eyebrow">PRÁCTICA · EJERCICIO ADICIONAL</span><h3>Pausa y resuelve</h3><p>${l.exercise}</p><details><summary>Ver explicación</summary><p>${l.solution}</p></details></section><section><span class="eyebrow">CONECTA LAS IDEAS</span><div class="chip-row">${l.connections.map(id=>lessons.find(x=>x.id===id)).filter(Boolean).map(x=>`<a class="chip" href="#/lesson/${x.id}">${x.title} ↗</a>`).join('')}</div></section><section class="source-section"><h3>Origen de esta lección</h3><p class="caption">Síntesis de las fuentes indicadas. Puedes exportar tus notas desde Material y programa.</p></section><div class="lesson-bottom"><button class="primary" id="complete" data-id="${id}">${state.done.includes(id)?'✓ Completada · desmarcar':'Marcar como comprendida'}</button>${next?`<a class="secondary" href="#/lesson/${next.id}">Siguiente: ${next.title} →</a>`:'<a class="secondary" href="#/practice">Repasar con un quiz →</a>'}</div></article><aside class="lesson-toc"><span class="eyebrow">EN ESTA LECCIÓN</span>${[['intuition','La idea'],['formula','Matemáticas'],['example','Paso a paso'],...(l.lab?[['experiment','Laboratorio']]:[]),['python','Python'],['mistakes','Errores frecuentes'],['exercise','Practica']].map(([id,label])=>`<button data-scroll="${id}">${label}</button>`).join('')}<div class="toc-note">No basta con leer.<br><strong>Predice, cambia algo y explica el resultado.</strong></div></aside></div>`;}
function concepts(){return heading('EXPLORAR CONCEPTOS','Encuentra la pieza que falta.','Busca por término, tema o pregunta. Cada concepto conecta con la lección y su fuente.')+`<label class="search-box"><span aria-hidden="true">⌕</span><input type="search" id="search" placeholder="Prueba «gradiente», «matrices», «overfitting»…" aria-label="Buscar conceptos" value="${escape(filter)}"></label><div id="search-results"></div>`;}
const normalized=s=>s.toLowerCase().normalize('NFD').replace(/[\u0300-\u036f]/g,'');
function results(){const found=lessons.filter(l=>normalized([l.title,l.subtitle,l.intuition,l.formula,l.symbols,...l.mistakes].join(' ')).includes(normalized(filter)));$('#search-results').innerHTML=`<p class="caption" role="status">${found.length} conceptos encontrados</p><div class="lesson-list">${found.map(lessonLink).join('')||'<div class="panel">No hay coincidencias. Prueba con otro término.</div>'}</div>`;}
function mathPage(){return heading('FUNDAMENTOS MATEMÁTICOS','El lenguaje de Machine Learning.','Repasa lo que necesitas ahora. Los temas avanzados del syllabus se añadirán cuando lleguen sus módulos.')+`<div class="three-grid">${[['Álgebra lineal','Vectores, matrices, dimensiones y productos.',['notation','numpy']],['Cálculo','Derivadas parciales, regla de la cadena y gradiente.',['loss','gradient']],['Estadística y geometría','Media, desviación, error y probabilidad por volumen.',['scaling','evaluation','dimensions']]].map(([title,desc,ids])=>`<section class="panel"><span class="math-symbol">${title==='Cálculo'?'∂':title==='Álgebra lineal'?'Xw':'σ'}</span><h3>${title}</h3><p>${desc}</p>${ids.map(id=>{const l=lessons.find(l=>l.id===id);return `<a class="recent" href="#/lesson/${id}">${l.title} ↗</a>`;}).join('')}</section>`).join('')}</div><div class="section-top"><h2>Repaso rápido</h2></div><div class="panel"><p>Producto punto: suma de productos por componente. Para vectores no nulos, x·w = ‖x‖₂‖w‖₂ cos(θ), donde θ es el ángulo entre ellos; si el producto es cero, son ortogonales.</p><p>Derivada parcial: cambia una variable manteniendo las otras fijas. El gradiente agrupa esas tasas de cambio en un vector.</p><p>Varianza: promedio de cuadrados de las desviaciones respecto a la media. Distingue la convención poblacional 1/n usada por StandardScaler de la estimación muestral 1/(n−1).</p><p class="caption">Syllabus, pág. 2, también enumera Bayes, eigenvectores y otras bases para el semestre. No se presentan aquí como temas ya desarrollados en las primeras dos semanas.</p></div>`;}
function codePage(){return heading('CÓDIGO CON CONTEXTO','De una idea a Python.','Ejemplos pequeños para ejecutar en Jupyter o Colab. Abre la lección para ver las dimensiones y la explicación línea por línea.')+`<p class="info-note">Los snippets son ejemplos de estudio y no se ejecutan aquí. Tu notebook de Assignment 1 permanece intacto. Los resultados mostrados como comentarios son cálculos esperados; consulta el informe de validación para los ejemplos ejecutados.</p>${lessons.map(l=>`<section class="code-section"><div class="section-top"><h3>${l.title}</h3><a href="#/lesson/${l.id}">Entender el código ↗</a></div><div class="code-block"><div><span>Semana ${l.week} · Python</span><button class="copy" data-copy="${l.id}">Copiar código</button></div><pre><code>${escape(l.code)}</code></pre></div><p>${l.codeNotes}</p></section>`).join('')}`;}
function labPage(id){return heading('APRENDER HACIENDO','El laboratorio.','Cambia una variable, observa el resultado y vuelve a la explicación.')+`<div class="lab-tabs" role="navigation" aria-label="Elegir laboratorio">${labs.map(([slug,title])=>`<a class="chip ${id===slug?'selected':''}" href="#/labs/${slug}">${title}</a>`).join('')}</div><div id="full-lab"></div><div class="panel bottom-grid"><h3>Del experimento a la idea</h3><div class="chip-row">${lessons.filter(l=>l.lab===id).map(l=>`<a href="#/lesson/${l.id}" class="chip">${l.title} ↗</a>`).join('')}</div></div>`;}
function practice(id){if(id)quizWeek=Number(id);const qs=quizzes.filter(q=>!quizWeek||q.week===quizWeek);const answered=qs.filter(q=>state.answers[q.id]!==undefined),correct=answered.filter(q=>state.answers[q.id]===q.answer);return heading('RECUPERAR · RAZONAR · REPETIR','Pon a prueba lo que entiendes.','Preguntas de práctica creadas para este portal a partir de los conceptos del curso. No son preguntas oficiales ni predicciones del examen.')+`<div class="practice-toolbar"><label>Filtrar preguntas <select id="quiz-week"><option value="0" ${quizWeek===0?'selected':''}>Ambas semanas</option><option value="1" ${quizWeek===1?'selected':''}>Semana 1</option><option value="2" ${quizWeek===2?'selected':''}>Semana 2</option></select></label><span>${correct.length} correctas · ${answered.length}/${qs.length} respondidas</span><button class="secondary" id="quiz-reset">Reintentar este bloque</button></div><section class="panel flashcard-panel"><div><span class="eyebrow">TARJETAS DE REPASO</span><span class="caption" id="flash-count"></span></div><div id="flashcard"></div><div class="button-row"><button class="secondary" id="flash-prev" aria-label="Tarjeta anterior">←</button><button class="primary" id="flash-flip">Mostrar respuesta</button><button class="secondary" id="flash-next" aria-label="Tarjeta siguiente">→</button></div></section>${qs.map((q,i)=>{const answer=state.answers[q.id],graded=answer!==undefined;return `<section class="quiz-card" id="${q.id}"><span class="eyebrow">PREGUNTA ${String(i+1).padStart(2,'0')} · SEMANA ${q.week}</span><h3>${q.q}</h3><div class="quiz-options">${q.options.map((opt,j)=>`<button class="quiz-option ${graded&&j===q.answer?'correct':''} ${graded&&answer===j&&j!==q.answer?'incorrect':''}" data-question="${q.id}" data-answer="${j}" ${graded?'disabled':''}><span>${'ABCD'[j]}</span>${opt}${graded&&j===q.answer?' ✓':''}</button>`).join('')}</div>${graded?`<div class="quiz-feedback" role="status"><strong>${answer===q.answer?'Correcto.':'Revisa esta idea.'}</strong> ${q.explanation} <a href="#/lesson/${q.lesson}">Volver a la lección ↗</a></div>`:''}</section>`;}).join('')}`;}
function flash(){const ls=lessons.filter(l=>!quizWeek||l.week===quizWeek),l=ls[flashIndex%ls.length];$('#flash-count').textContent=`${flashIndex%ls.length+1} / ${ls.length}`;$('#flashcard').innerHTML=`<h3>${l.title}</h3><p>${flashBack?l.solution:l.exercise}</p>`;$('#flash-flip').textContent=flashBack?'Mostrar pregunta':'Mostrar respuesta';}
function cheats(){return heading('CONSULTA RÁPIDA','Tu hoja de fórmulas.','Símbolos, condiciones y recordatorios para volver a lo esencial.')+`<button class="secondary print-button" id="print">Imprimir / guardar PDF</button><p class="info-note">Material de estudio generado con ayuda de IA. El syllabus exige que las hojas permitidas en los Professional Quizzes sean preparadas únicamente por el estudiante; crea tu propia versión según las instrucciones de Canvas.</p>${lessons.filter(l=>!['bias','numpy','iris','generalization','supervised','batches'].includes(l.id)).map(l=>`<section class="formula-card"><h3>${l.title}</h3><div class="formula">${math(l.formula)}</div><p>${l.symbols}</p><a href="#/lesson/${l.id}">Ver derivación y ejemplo →</a></section>`).join('')}`;}
function sourcePage(id){return heading('FUENTES Y ORGANIZACIÓN','Un curso que va creciendo.','15 archivos inventariados. El contenido distingue los notebooks, las tutorías, las lecturas complementarias y las explicaciones adicionales.')+`<div class="two-grid"><section class="panel"><h3>Tu progreso es tuyo</h3><p>Las notas, respuestas y lecciones comprendidas se guardan en este navegador. Exporta una copia para llevarlas a otro equipo.</p><div class="button-row"><button class="primary" id="export">Exportar progreso y notas</button><label class="secondary file-label">Importar copia<input type="file" id="import" accept="application/json,.json"></label></div><p class="caption">El guardado no se sincroniza entre dispositivos ni con el VPS.</p></section><section class="panel"><h3>Cómo añadir la siguiente semana</h3><p>Sube sus materiales a ML NJIT. Los PDFs completos, las transcripciones con participantes y tu entrega personal permanecen en el proyecto local. Hay respuestas en las preguntas 1–5. Esto no es una calificación ni una confirmación de entrega. El código base de voto kNN usa <code>c &gt;= k//2</code>, que no representa mayoría estricta; tu copia conserva esa condición. Revisa también los tipos numéricos en histogramas y filtros del dataset.</p><h3>Material pendiente o externo</h3><p>Las carpetas Ejemplos y Kaggle están vacías. Las listas contienen títulos, no URLs de las clases: no se han inventado enlaces de video. El notebook de código de regresión enlazado en §3.12 no está en los archivos locales. Los libros incluyen temas futuros, que no se han marcado como cursados.</p></section><div class="section-top"><h2>Programa del semestre</h2><span>Según syllabus, págs. 5–6</span></div><div class="panel">${schedule.map(([w,title])=>`<div class="schedule-row"><span>Semana ${w}</span><strong>${title}</strong><small>Material pendiente</small></div>`).join('')}<p class="caption">La semana de Thanksgiving no cuenta en la numeración del programa. Canvas determina las fechas operativas y posibles cambios; no se ha consultado tu calendario privado.</p></div><section class="panel bottom-grid"><h3>Evaluación del curso</h3><p>Participación 10% · Theory Reviews 10% · Assignments 25% · Mini-Project 25% · Mid-Semester Quiz 10% · End-of-Semester Quiz 20%.</p><p>Assignment 1: semana 2 (2.5%). Project Milestone 1: semana 3 (3%). Los demás hitos del proyecto pesan 6%, 1% y 15%. Revisa las rúbricas y las fechas vigentes en Canvas.</p><h3>Documentación técnica de apoyo</h3><p class="caption">APIs contrastadas con documentación oficial; son referencias adicionales, no material de clase.</p><div class="chip-row">${references.map(([t,url])=>`<a class="chip" href="${url}" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">${t} ↗</a>`).join('')}</div></section>`;}
const missing=()=>heading('PÁGINA NO ENCONTRADA','Volvamos al cuaderno.','Este tema no existe o todavía no se ha añadido.')+'<a href="#/" class="primary">Ir al inicio</a>';
function render(){
 const [page='',id='']=location.hash.replace(/^#\/?/,'').split('/');document.body.classList.remove('menu-open');$('#menu').setAttribute('aria-expanded','false');
 const names={'':'Inicio',week:'El curso',lesson:'Lección',concepts:'Explorar conceptos',math:'Bases matemáticas',code:'Ejemplos Python',labs:'Laboratorios',practice:'Practicar',cheats:'Hoja de fórmulas',sources:'Material y programa'};
 $('#crumb').textContent=names[page]||'Página no encontrada';document.title=`${names[page]||'DS675'} · DS675 Machine Learning`;
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-nav]').forEach(a=>{const key=a.dataset.nav;const active=key===(page?`${page}${id?'/'+id:''}`:'')||key.split('/')[0]===page&&!['week',''].includes(page)||page==='lesson'&&key==='week/'+lessons.find(l=>l.id===id)?.week;a.classList.toggle('active',active);if(active)a.setAttribute('aria-current','page');else a.removeAttribute('aria-current');});
 $('#main').innerHTML=page==='week'?weekPage(id):page==='lesson'?lessonPage(id):page==='concepts'?concepts():page==='math'?mathPage():page==='code'?codePage():page==='labs'?labPage(id||'knn'):page==='practice'?practice(id):page==='cheats'?cheats():page==='sources'?sourcePage(id):page===''?dashboard():missing();
 if(page==='concepts'){results();$('#search').oninput=e=>{filter=e.target.value;results();};}
 if(page==='labs')mountLab($('#full-lab'),id||'knn');
 const l=lessons.find(l=>l.id===id);if(page==='lesson'&&l){if(l.lab)mountLab($('#embedded-lab'),l.lab);$('#lesson-note').oninput=e=>{state.notes[id]=e.target.value;save();$('#note-status').textContent='Notas guardadas en este navegador.';};$('#complete').onclick=()=>{state.done=state.done.includes(id)?state.done.filter(x=>x!==id):[...state.done,id];save();$('#complete').textContent=state.done.includes(id)?'✓ Completada · desmarcar':'Marcar como comprendida';};}
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-copy]').forEach(b=>b.onclick=async()=>{try{await navigator.clipboard.writeText(lessons.find(l=>l.id===b.dataset.copy).code);toast('Código copiado.');}catch{toast('Selecciona el código y cópialo con Ctrl+C.');}});
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-scroll]').forEach(b=>b.onclick=()=>document.getElementById(b.dataset.scroll).scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'}));
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-check]').forEach(c=>c.onchange=()=>{state.checks=c.checked?[...new Set([...state.checks,c.dataset.check])]:state.checks.filter(x=>x!==c.dataset.check);save();});
 if(page==='practice'){
  flash();$('#quiz-week').onchange=e=>{quizWeek=Number(e.target.value);if(location.hash==='#/practice')render();else location.hash='/practice';};$('#quiz-reset').onclick=()=>{quizzes.filter(q=>!quizWeek||q.week===quizWeek).forEach(q=>delete state.answers[q.id]);save();render();};
  $('#flash-flip').onclick=()=>{flashBack=!flashBack;flash();};$('#flash-next').onclick=()=>{flashIndex++;flashBack=false;flash();};$('#flash-prev').onclick=()=>{const n=lessons.filter(l=>!quizWeek||l.week===quizWeek).length;flashIndex=(flashIndex%n+n-1)%n;flashBack=false;flash();};
  document.querySelectorAll('[data-question]').forEach(b=>b.onclick=()=>{const y=window.scrollY;state.answers[b.dataset.question]=Number(b.dataset.answer);save();render();window.scrollTo(0,y);});
 }
 if($('#print'))$('#print').onclick=()=>window.print();
 if($('#export')){
  $('#export').onclick=()=>{const blob=new Blob([JSON.stringify(state,null,2)],{type:'application/json'}),url=URL.createObjectURL(blob),a=document.createElement('a');a.href=url;a.download='ds675-progreso-'+new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)+'.json';a.click();URL.revokeObjectURL(url);};
  $('#import').onchange=async e=>{try{const file=e.target.files[0];if(!file||file.size>2e6)throw Error();const data=JSON.parse(await file.text());if(data.version!==1||!Array.isArray(data.done)||!data.notes||typeof data.notes!=='object'||!data.answers||typeof data.answers!=='object')throw Error();const valid=new Set(lessons.map(l=>l.id));state.done=[...new Set([...state.done,...data.done.filter(x=>valid.has(x))])];for(const [id,text] of Object.entries(data.notes))if(valid.has(id)&&typeof text==='string'&&text.length<=50000)state.notes[id]=text;for(const q of quizzes)if(Number.isInteger(data.answers[q.id])&&data.answers[q.id]>=0&&data.answers[q.id]<q.options.length)state.answers[q.id]=data.answers[q.id];state.checks=[...new Set([...state.checks,...(Array.isArray(data.checks)?data.checks.filter(x=>/^w[12]-[0-3]$/.test(x)):[])])];save();toast('Copia importada. Se combinaron lecciones y se restauraron las notas y respuestas incluidas.');}catch{toast('No se pudo importar: selecciona una copia válida de DS675 (máximo 2 MB).');}e.target.value='';};
 }
 if(page==='sources'&&id)document.getElementById('source-'+id)?.scrollIntoView({block:'center'});
}
shell();render();window.addEventListener('hashchange',()=>{render();if(!location.hash.startsWith('#/sources/'))window.scrollTo(0,0);$('#main').focus({preventScroll:true});});

function results(){const found=lessons.filter(l=>normalized([l.title,l.subtitle,l.intuition,l.formula,l.symbols,...l.mistakes].join(' ')).includes(normalized(filter)));$('#search-results').innerHTML=`<p class="caption" role="status">${found.length} conceptos encontrados</p><div class="lesson-list">${found.map(lessonLink).join('')||'<div class="panel">No hay coincidencias. function mathPage(){return heading('FUNDAMENTOS MATEMÁTICOS','El lenguaje de Machine Learning.','Repasa lo que necesitas ahora. Los temas avanzados del syllabus se añadirán cuando lleguen sus módulos.')+`<div class="three-grid">${[['Álgebra lineal','Vectores, matrices, dimensiones y productos.',['notation','numpy']],['Cálculo','Derivadas parciales, regla de la cadena y gradiente.',['loss','gradient']],['Estadística y geometría','Media, desviación, error y probabilidad por volumen.',['scaling','evaluation','dimensions']]].map(([title,desc,ids])=>`<section class="panel"><span class="math-symbol">${title==='Cálculo'?'∂':title==='Álgebra lineal'?'Xw':'σ'}</span><h3>${title}</h3><p>${desc}</p>${ids.map(id=>{const l=lessons.find(l=>l.id===id);return `<a class="recent" href="#/lesson/${id}">${l.title} ↗</a>`;}).join('')}</section>`).join('')}</div><div class="section-top"><h2>Repaso rápido</h2></div><div class="panel"><p>Producto punto: suma de productos por componente. Para vectores no nulos, x·w = ‖x‖₂‖w‖₂ cos(θ), donde θ es el ángulo entre ellos; si el producto es cero, son ortogonales.</p><p>Derivada parcial: cambia una variable manteniendo las otras fijas. El gradiente agrupa esas tasas de cambio en un vector.</p><p>Varianza: promedio de cuadrados de las desviaciones respecto a la media. Distingue la convención poblacional 1/n usada por StandardScaler de la estimación muestral 1/(n−1).</p><p class="caption">Syllabus, pág. 2, también enumera Bayes, eigenvectores y otras bases para el semestre. No se presentan aquí como temas ya desarrollados en las primeras dos semanas.</p></div>`;}

function practice(id){if(id)quizWeek=Number(id);const qs=quizzes.filter(q=>!quizWeek||q.week===quizWeek);const answered=qs.filter(q=>state.answers[q.id]!==undefined),correct=answered.filter(q=>state.answers[q.id]===q.answer);return heading('RECUPERAR · RAZONAR · REPETIR','Pon a prueba lo que entiendes.','Preguntas de práctica creadas para este portal a partir de los conceptos del curso. function flash(){const ls=lessons.filter(l=>!quizWeek||l.week===quizWeek),l=ls[flashIndex%ls.length];$('#flash-count').textContent=`${flashIndex%ls.length+1} / ${ls.length}`;$('#flashcard').innerHTML=`<h3>${l.title}</h3><p>${flashBack?l.solution:l.exercise}</p>`;$('#flash-flip').textContent=flashBack?'Mostrar pregunta':'Mostrar respuesta';}
function cheats(){return heading('CONSULTA RÁPIDA','Tu hoja de fórmulas.','Símbolos, condiciones y recordatorios para volver a lo esencial.')+`<button class="secondary print-button" id="print">Imprimir / guardar PDF</button><p class="info-note">Material de estudio generado con ayuda de IA. function sourcePage(id){return heading('FUENTES Y ORGANIZACIÓN','Un curso que va creciendo.','15 archivos inventariados. function render(){
 const [page='',id='']=location.hash.replace(/^#\/?/,'').split('/');document.body.classList.remove('menu-open');$('#menu').setAttribute('aria-expanded','false');
 const names={'':'Inicio',week:'El curso',lesson:'Lección',concepts:'Explorar conceptos',math:'Bases matemáticas',code:'Ejemplos Python',labs:'Laboratorios',practice:'Practicar',cheats:'Hoja de fórmulas',sources:'Material y programa'};
 $('#crumb').textContent=names[page]||'Página no encontrada';document.title=`${names[page]||'DS675'} · DS675 Machine Learning`;
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-nav]').forEach(a=>{const key=a.dataset.nav;const active=key===(page?`${page}${id?'/'+id:''}`:'')||key.split('/')[0]===page&&!['week',''].includes(page)||page==='lesson'&&key==='week/'+lessons.find(l=>l.id===id)?.week;a.classList.toggle('active',active);if(active)a.setAttribute('aria-current','page');else a.removeAttribute('aria-current');});
 $('#main').innerHTML=page==='week'?weekPage(id):page==='lesson'?lessonPage(id):page==='concepts'?concepts():page==='math'?mathPage():page==='code'?codePage():page==='labs'?labPage(id||'knn'):page==='practice'?practice(id):page==='cheats'?cheats():page==='sources'?sourcePage(id):page===''?dashboard():missing();
 if(page==='concepts'){results();$('#search').oninput=e=>{filter=e.target.value;results();};}
 if(page==='labs')mountLab($('#full-lab'),id||'knn');
 const l=lessons.find(l=>l.id===id);if(page==='lesson'&&l){if(l.lab)mountLab($('#embedded-lab'),l.lab);$('#lesson-note').oninput=e=>{state.notes[id]=e.target.value;save();$('#note-status').textContent='Notas guardadas en este navegador.';};$('#complete').onclick=()=>{state.done=state.done.includes(id)?state.done.filter(x=>x!==id):[...state.done,id];save();$('#complete').textContent=state.done.includes(id)?'✓ Completada · desmarcar':'Marcar como comprendida';};}
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-copy]').forEach(b=>b.onclick=async()=>{try{await navigator.clipboard.writeText(lessons.find(l=>l.id===b.dataset.copy).code);toast('Código copiado.');}catch{toast('Selecciona el código y cópialo con Ctrl+C.');}});
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-scroll]').forEach(b=>b.onclick=()=>document.getElementById(b.dataset.scroll).scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth'}));
 document.querySelectorAll('[data-check]').forEach(c=>c.onchange=()=>{state.checks=c.checked?[...new Set([...state.checks,c.dataset.check])]:state.checks.filter(x=>x!==c.dataset.check);save();});
 if(page==='practice'){
  flash();$('#quiz-week').onchange=e=>{quizWeek=Number(e.target.value);if(location.hash==='#/practice')render();else location.hash='/practice';};$('#quiz-reset').onclick=()=>{quizzes.filter(q=>!quizWeek||q.week===quizWeek).forEach(q=>delete state.answers[q.id]);save();render();};
  $('#flash-flip').onclick=()=>{flashBack=!flashBack;flash();};$('#flash-next').onclick=()=>{flashIndex++;flashBack=false;flash();};$('#flash-prev').onclick=()=>{const n=lessons.filter(l=>!quizWeek||l.week===quizWeek).length;flashIndex=(flashIndex%n+n-1)%n;flashBack=false;flash();};
  document.querySelectorAll('[data-question]').forEach(b=>b.onclick=()=>{const y=window.scrollY;state.answers[b.dataset.question]=Number(b.dataset.answer);save();render();window.scrollTo(0,y);});
 }
 if($('#print'))$('#print').onclick=()=>window.print();
 if($('#export')){
  $('#export').onclick=()=>{const blob=new Blob([JSON.stringify(state,null,2)],{type:'application/json'}),url=URL.createObjectURL(blob),a=document.createElement('a');a.href=url;a.download='ds675-progreso-'+new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)+'.json';a.click();URL.revokeObjectURL(url);};
  $('#import').onchange=async e=>{try{const file=e.target.files[0];if(!file||file.size>2e6)throw Error();const data=JSON.parse(await file.text());if(data.version!==1||!Array.isArray(data.done)||!data.notes||typeof data.notes!=='object'||!data.answers||typeof data.answers!=='object')throw Error();const valid=new Set(lessons.map(l=>l.id));state.done=[...new Set([...state.done,...data.done.filter(x=>valid.has(x))])];for(const [id,text] of Object.entries(data.notes))if(valid.has(id)&&typeof text==='string'&&text.length<=50000)state.notes[id]=text;for(const q of quizzes)if(Number.isInteger(data.answers[q.id])&&data.answers[q.id]>=0&&data.answers[q.id]<q.options.length)state.answers[q.id]=data.answers[q.id];state.checks=[...new Set([...state.checks,...(Array.isArray(data.checks)?data.checks.filter(x=>/^w[12]-[0-3]$/.test(x)):[])])];save();toast('Copia importada. Se combinaron lecciones y se restauraron las notas y respuestas incluidas.');}catch{toast('No se pudo importar: selecciona una copia válida de DS675 (máximo 2 MB).');}e.target.value='';};
 }
 if(page==='sources'&&id)document.getElementById('source-'+id)?.scrollIntoView({block:'center'});
}
shell();render();window.addEventListener('hashchange',()=>{render();if(!location.hash.startsWith('#/sources/'))window.scrollTo(0,0);$('#main').focus({preventScroll:true});});

Una decisión común usa p̂>0.5.',\n+['Si z=0, σ(z)=0.5: el modelo está en la frontera.','Si z=2, la probabilidad es aproximadamente 0.881; si z=−2, aproximadamente 0.119.','La clase se obtiene después de elegir un umbral. La probabilidad conserva más información que la clase final.'],\n+`import numpy as np\n+def sigmoid(z):\n+    return 1 / (1 + np.exp(-z))\n+for z in [-2, 0, 2]:\n+    print(z, sigmoid(z), int(sigmoid(z) > 0.5))`,\n+'La comparación con 0.5 convierte la salida continua en decisión. El umbral exacto forma parte de la regla de decisión.',\n+['Llamar regresión al tipo de target por el nombre “logistic regression”.','Confundir probabilidad con certeza.','Aplicar el umbral antes de calcular la pérdida de entrenamiento.'],\n+'¿Qué probabilidad produce z=0 y por qué ese punto está en la frontera?',\n+'σ(0)=0.5. El logit toma el logaritmo de esa razón y puede recorrer toda la recta real.',\n+'La regresión logística supone que ese log-odds es una combinación lineal de las entradas. Al despejar p aparece exactamente la sigmoide.',\n+'\\log\\frac{p}{1-p}=z=w^\\top x+b\\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad p=\\frac1{1+e^{-z}}',\n+'p/(1−p) son los odds; log(p/(1−p)) es el logit. z puede ser cualquier real aunque p solo esté entre 0 y 1.',\n+['Si p=0.5, odds=1 y logit=0.','Si p=0.8, odds=4 y logit≈1.386.','La frontera p=0.5 sigue siendo wᵀx+b=0; por eso el clasificador logístico básico también tiene una frontera lineal.'],\n+`import numpy as np\n+p = 0.8\n+odds = p / (1-p)\n+logit = np.log(odds)\n+print(odds, logit, 1/(1+np.exp(-logit)))`,\n+'El último cálculo invierte el logit y recupera p.',\n+['Confundir odds con probabilidad.','Decir que la sigmoide vuelve no lineal la frontera básica.','Calcular logit en p=0 o p=1 sin considerar que diverge.'],\n+'Si los odds son 3, ¿cuánto vale p?',\n+'p/(1−p)=3 implica p=3/4=0.75.', ['logistic-neuron','separability','logistic-loss'],'sigmoid'),\n+lesson('logistic-loss',4,5,'Premiar probabilidades correctas','Likelihood y negative log-likelihood',20,[['w4log','V03 · The loss function']],\n+'La likelihood asigna un valor alto cuando el modelo da alta probabilidad a la etiqueta observada. Para entrenar, se transforma su producto en una suma y se cambia el signo.',\n+'La pérdida castiga mucho una predicción segura y equivocada. Además es continua y diferenciable, por lo que se puede minimizar con gradiente.',\n+'\\ell(y,\\hat p)=-[y\\log\\hat p+(1-y)\\log(1-\\hat p)]',\n+'y vale 0 o 1; p̂ es la probabilidad predicha de clase 1.
